1: #!/usr/bin/env python3
2: """Export the sample deck to dist/anki-japanese-template.apkg via Anki-Connect.
3: 
4: Called by finish.sh; can also be run standalone. The real Anki-Connect action
5: is `exportPackage` (params: deck, path, includeSched). The apkg is gitignored
6: (`*.apkg` in .gitignore) — it is distributed via GitHub Releases, never the
7: repo itself.
8: """
9: import os
10: import sys
11: import json
12: import urllib.request
13: import urllib.error
14: 
15: ANKI_CONNECT_URL = "http://127.0.0.1:8765"
16: DECK_NAME = "My Life Decks::Japanese::anki-japanese-template"
17: SCRIPT_DIR = os.path.dirname(os.path.abspath(__file__))
18: EXPORT_PATH = os.path.join(SCRIPT_DIR, "dist", "anki-japanese-template.apkg")
19: 
20: 
21: def main() -> int:
22:     os.makedirs(os.path.dirname(EXPORT_PATH), exist_ok=True)
23:     payload = {
24:         "action": "exportPackage",
25:         "version": 6,
26:         "params": {"deck": DECK_NAME, "path": EXPORT_PATH, "includeSched": False},
27:     }
28:     
29:     req = urllib.request.Request(
30:         ANKI_CONNECT_URL,
31:         data=json.dumps(payload).encode("utf-8"),
32:         headers={"Content-Type": "application/json"},
33:     )
34:     
35:     try:
36:         with urllib.request.urlopen(req, timeout=180) as response:
37:             result = json.loads(response.read().decode("utf-8"))
38:     except urllib.error.URLError:
39:         print("ERROR: cannot reach Anki-Connect (is Anki running with the add-on?)")
40:         return 1
41:     except Exception as e:
42:         print(f"ERROR: export request failed: {e}")
43:         return 1
44: 
45:     if result.get("error") or not result.get("result"):
46:         print(f"ERROR: Anki-Connect export failed: {result.get('error') or 'unknown error'}")
47:         return 1
48: 
49:     if not os.path.isfile(EXPORT_PATH) or os.path.getsize(EXPORT_PATH) == 0:
50:         print(f"ERROR: expected export missing or empty: {EXPORT_PATH}")
51:         return 1
52: 
53:     print(f"Export OK: {EXPORT_PATH} ({os.path.getsize(EXPORT_PATH) / 1e6:.1f} MB)")
54:     return 0
55: 
56: 
57: if __name__ == "__main__":
58:     sys.exit(main())

(End of file - total 58 lines)
</content>

<type>file</type>
<content>
1: #!/usr/bin/env python3
2: """Fetch live note-type field names + descriptions from Anki (read-only).
3: 
4: Fields are managed EXCLUSIVELY inside the Anki UI — this repo keeps no
5: static field list. Agents must run this script at session start (see
6: AGENTS.md rule 0) and read the generated `.anki_fields.json` snapshot
7: instead of guessing field names.
8: 
9: Usage:  python3 fetch_anki_fields.py
10: Output: .anki_fields.json (gitignored) + the field list on stdout.
11: 
12: Requires: Anki running with the Anki-Connect add-on.
13: Standard library only (mirrors sync_to_anki.py).
14: """
15: 
16: import json
17: import os
18: import sys
19: import urllib.request
20: 
21: # Keep in sync with sync_to_anki.py
22: ANKI_CONNECT_URL = "http://127.0.0.1:8765"
23: MODEL_NAME = "Japanese Note type (Sentence card by Default)"
24: 
25: SCRIPT_DIR = os.path.dirname(os.path.abspath(__file__))
26: OUT_FILE = os.path.join(SCRIPT_DIR, ".anki_fields.json")
27: 
28: 
29: def _anki(action, **params):
30:     req = urllib.request.Request(
31:         ANKI_CONNECT_URL,
32:         data=json.dumps({"action": action, "version": 6, "params": params}).encode("utf-8"),
33:         headers={"Content-Type": "application/json"},
34:     )
35:     try:
36:         with urllib.request.urlopen(req, timeout=10) as response:
37:             result = json.loads(response.read().decode("utf-8"))
38:     except Exception as e:
39:         print(f"ERROR: Could not reach Anki-Connect at {ANKI_CONNECT_URL}. "
40:               f"Is Anki running with the add-on installed? ({e})")
41:         sys.exit(1)
42:     if result.get("error"):
43:         print(f"ERROR: Anki-Connect returned: {result['error']}")
44:         sys.exit(1)
45:     return result.get("result")
46: 
47: 
48: def _anki_soft(action, **params):
49:     """Like _anki but returns None instead of exiting (optional data)."""
50:     req = urllib.request.Request(
51:         ANKI_CONNECT_URL,
52:         data=json.dumps({"action": action, "version": 6, "params": params}).encode("utf-8"),
53:         headers={"Content-Type": "application/json"},
54:     )
55:     try:
56:         with urllib.request.urlopen(req, timeout=10) as response:
57:             result = json.loads(response.read().decode("utf-8"))
58:     except Exception:
59:         return None
60:     return result.get("result") if not result.get("error") else None
61: 
62: 
63: def main():
64:     fields = _anki("modelFieldNames", modelName=MODEL_NAME)
65:     if not isinstance(fields, list) or not fields:
66:         print(f"ERROR: no fields returned for model '{MODEL_NAME}' — does it still exist?")
67:         sys.exit(1)
68:     # Descriptions are typed by the user in Anki (Fields dialog). Older
69:     # Anki-Connect builds lack this action — degrade to names only.
70:     descriptions = _anki_soft("modelFieldDescriptions", modelName=MODEL_NAME)
71:     if not isinstance(descriptions, list) or len(descriptions) != len(fields):
72:         descriptions = [""] * len(fields)
73:     entries = [
74:         {"name": name, "description": desc or ""}
75:         for name, desc in zip(fields, descriptions)
76:     ]
77:     with open(OUT_FILE, "w", encoding="utf-8") as f:
78:         json.dump({"model": MODEL_NAME, "fields": entries}, f, ensure_ascii=False, indent=2)
79:         f.write("\n")
80:     print(f"Model: {MODEL_NAME} ({len(entries)} fields) -> .anki_fields.json")
81:     for i, entry in enumerate(entries):
82:         line = f"  {i:2d}. {entry['name']}"
83:         if entry["description"]:
84:             line += f" — {entry['description']}"
85:         print(line)
86: 
87: 
88: if __name__ == "__main__":
89:     main()

(End of file - total 89 lines)
</content>
```

{
  "content": "# PRODUCT.md — Japanese sentence-mining note type\n\nAuthoritative source for **what the product is supposed to do**.\nArchitecture, plans, and agent instructions all defer to this file.\n\n## Problem\n\nYomitan sentence-mining cards are verbose and ergonomically poor out of the\nbox: oversized headwords, full multi-dictionary glosses, dead screen space,\nclunky audio controls, and a familiar sentence that becomes the retrieval cue\nonce a card is mature (overlearning). Review on a small phone (Galaxy A50) is\nespecially wasteful.\n\n## Target users\n\n- Solo learner (the maintainer) mining Japanese sentences via Yomitan /\n  jidoujisho into Anki.\n- Review contexts: Anki Desktop Qt6 WebEngine (Arch Linux, widescreen) and\n  AnkiDroid WebView (Samsung Galaxy A50, small screen).\n- Installs via the released `.apkg` (GitHub Release asset), or syncs from\n  source with Anki-Connect.\n\n## Core user journeys\n\n1. **Mine → review sentence card.** Yomitan fills Expression / Sentence /\n   Definition / audio / Frequency; the front tests sentence recognition, the\n   back shows word, badges, definition, sentence, translation, media, notes.\n2. **Review mature card as word card.** Cards with interval ≥\n   `LONG_INTERVAL_DAYS` show only the Expression on the front\n   (anti-overlearning), with silent fallback to the sentence front.\n3. **Review listening card.** Cards with audio but no definitions test\n   recognition via a single large audio button.\n4. **Expand media / definition / translation on demand.** Picture lightbox,\n   3-line definition truncation with one-way expand, click-to-reveal\n   translation, extended-definition accordion.\n5. **Install / update.** Import the release `.apkg`, or push local\n   templates/CSS to the live profile and re-export via `finish.sh`.\n\n## Functional requirements\n\n- Front modes (in priority order): Definition/Extended-definition →\n  sentence; legacy Frequency-only → sentence; audio-only → listening button;\n  otherwise sentence/Expression fallback; cloze trio rebuild when Sentence\n  lacks `<b>`; mature interval-gated word-only front.\n- Back: word/furigana header, frequency + pitch badges, word + sentence\n  circular audio, compacted primary definition, sentence + translation +\n  context, picture / kanji / general notes, extended-definition accordion,\n  source footer, sticky tags bar.\n- Mature Word Mode: live interval read at render time (desktop AnkiConnect\n  `guiCurrentCard`→`cardsInfo` + `findCards` content-search fallback for the\n  Browse previewer; mobile AnkiDroid JS API `ankiGetCardInterval()` only);\n  never fetch AnkiConnect from a mobile WebView; any failure → sentence\n  front; listening fronts untouched.\n- Tooling: `sync_to_anki.py` pushes Front/Back/CSS with a pre-sync snapshot\n  to `backups/<timestamp>/`; `release_apkg.py` exports deck\n  `My Life Decks::Japanese::anki-japanese-template` via `exportPackage`;\n  `finish.sh` runs tests → sync → export → commit → push → release.\n\n## UX requirements\n\n- Ultra-compact, content-driven height (no viewport fill, no dead space).\n- Fluid `clamp()` sizing phone → 4K; 2-column back grid on desktop,\n  1-column on phones (container queries + media-query fallback).\n- Zero-reflow furigana (hidden, hover/tap reveal, absolute ruby).\n- Native-only audio (delegate to Anki replay link, re-tap debounce, ring\n  pulse — never HTML5 `Audio`).\n- Dual themes (Tokyo Night dark / Aki Paper light, follows Anki Night Mode),\n  decorative Fuji backdrop (deletable block), `prefers-reduced-motion`\n  support, keyboard focus indicators, aria labels/roles.\n\n## Constraints\n\n- Vanilla JS/CSS only, scoped, resilient to Anki WebView DOM re-use.\n- Fields live **exclusively in the Anki UI**; repo keeps no static field\n  list (`fetch_anki_fields.py` → gitignored `.anki_fields.json` is the only\n  source).\n- Local `.template.anki` / `.css` files are the single source of truth —\n  never edit inside the Anki UI.\n- Screen real estate is precious: no debug badges or verbose header labels.\n- Stdlib-only Python tooling (no third-party runtime deps; test-only deps\n  allowed).\n\n## Non-goals\n\n- New Anki note-type fields, add-ons, or Python-at-review solutions.\n- Multi-note-type theming system or generic card framework.\n- Enterprise release management (solo `finish.sh` flow is enough).\n- Server/cloud sync, collaboration, analytics.\n\n## Important assumptions\n\n- Yomitan markup shape (`.yomitan-glossary`, `data-sc-*` attributes) is\n  stable enough for structural CSS scoping; drift is caught by fixtures.\n- AnkiConnect on `127.0.0.1:8765` (desktop) and the AnkiDroid JS bridge\n  (reviewer only) remain the interval sources; both can be absent.\n- One card type (\"Card 1\"); model name\n  `Japanese Note type (Sentence card by Default)` is stable.\n\n## Open questions\n\n- None blocking. Candidate evolution items live in `MVP.md` (Deferred) and\n  `IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md` (roadmap).\n",
  "filePath": "/home/mohamed/Desktop/Github/anki-japanese-template/PRODUCT.md"
}

{
  "content": "# MVP.md — current scope\n\n`PRODUCT.md` answers *what is this product?* This file answers *what are we\nactually building right now?* Agents must not independently widen this scope\nwhile coding.\n\n## MVP goal\n\nA shippable, review-ready Japanese sentence-mining note type: dense on\ndesktop, usable on a small phone, with sentence/word/listening fronts, a\ncompact informative back, and a one-command sync → export → release loop.\n\n## Core user journey(s)\n\n1. Import the release `.apkg` → review mined cards daily on desktop and phone.\n2. Mature cards automatically test the word, not the memorized sentence.\n3. Edit templates/CSS locally → `./finish.sh` → cards updated in Anki and a\n   new tagged release published.\n\n## Included capabilities\n\n- Front: sentence / Expression fallback, listening-mode audio button,\n  Frequency-legacy sentence path, cloze-trio rebuild, Mature Word Mode\n  (interval-gated, platform-exclusive retrieval, sentence fallback).\n- Back: sticky tags, word + furigana header, frequency/pitch badges, native\n  circular audio, Definition Compactor (CSS §6b), 3-line truncator with\n  one-way expand (§6c), sentence + translation toggle, picture lightbox,\n  kanji/general notes, extended-definition accordion, source footer.\n- Styling: Tokyo Night + Aki Paper themes, fluid clamps, container-query\n  2-column grid with media fallback, zero-reflow furigana, Fuji backdrop,\n  reduced-motion support.\n- Tooling: field bootstrap (`fetch_anki_fields.py`), snapshotted sync\n  (`sync_to_anki.py`), apkg export (`release_apkg.py`), `verify` gate,\n  `finish.sh` release loop, regression tests.\n\n## Excluded capabilities\n\n- New fields, add-ons, or review-time Python.\n- Additional card types or note types.\n- Cloud sync / collaboration / analytics.\n- Mandatory per-project doc/diagram checklists beyond `ARCHITECTURE.md`.\n\n## Acceptance criteria\n\n- `./verify` passes (compactor + template invariants; layout checks when\n  Chrome is present).\n- A real user can complete: import → review sentence card → review mature\n  word card → review listening card → expand definition/translation/image →\n  edit locally → `./finish.sh` → updated cards + tagged release.\n- No horizontal overflow, no viewport-fill dead space, furigana never shifts\n  layout, audio never overlaps, failures always degrade to the sentence front.\n\n## Known limitations\n\n- Mature interval needs AnkiConnect (desktop) or the AnkiDroid reviewer\n  bridge; Browse/template previews without a bridge correctly fall back to\n  the sentence front.\n- AnkiDroid fragment-reviewer stub bridges (`signal:jsapi`) are detected and\n  never invoked.\n- Headless layout checks skip gracefully when Chrome is absent.\n\n## Deferred features\n\n- Further density passes beyond the current clamp/container-query system.\n- Additional AnkiDroid bridge capabilities (beyond interval read).\n- Extra dictionaries/markup variants beyond current compactor fixtures.\n- Any `docs/adr/` entries beyond decided items (added only when a decision\n  has alternatives + consequences + long-term relevance).\n",

{
  "content": "# ARCHITECTURE.md — authoritative technical structure\n\nCovers *how the software is structured* for the `MVP.md` scope. Additional\ndocuments are created only where they would otherwise make this file unwieldy\nor where a domain needs an independently maintained contract. Current extras:\n`docs/adr/` (decisions with lasting consequences); no separate\n`DATA_MODEL.md` / `API_CONTRACTS.md` / `STATE_MODEL.md` — the project is too\nsmall to justify them.\n\n## System map\n\n```text\nAnki note fields (live in Anki UI, never in repo)\n  │  Yomitan / jidoujisho mining\n  ↓\nCard 1 - Front.template.anki ──→ review front (sentence / word / listening)\nCard 1 - Back.template.anki  ──→ review back (grid: main + side columns)\nCard 1 - Style.css           ──→ themes, layout, compactor, truncator\n  │  vanilla JS (scoped, DOM-reuse safe), no libraries\n  ↓\nAnki renderers: Desktop Qt6 WebEngine · AnkiDroid WebView\n  │  live services at render time: AnkiConnect :8765 (desktop only),\n  │  AnkiDroid JS bridge (mobile reviewer only)\n  ↓\nTooling (stdlib-only): fetch_anki_fields.py · sync_to_anki.py ·\n                       release_apkg.py · verify · finish.sh\n```\n\n## Components\n\n### Front (`Card 1 - Front.template.anki`)\n\nHTML conditionals select the mode; JS finalizes it at render time:\n\n```text\nDefinition / Extended definition? ──yes──→ sentence-display\n        │ no\nFrequency (legacy)? ──yes──→ sentence-display (never audio)\n        │ no\nSentence Audio? ──yes──→ listening-view (large 文 button)\n        │ no\nsentence-display fallback (Sentence → Expression)\n        │\ncloze fixup: Sentence has no <b> AND cloze trio complete?\n        │ yes → prefix + <b>body</b> + suffix\n        │\nMature check (not listening, Expression non-empty,\n              interval ≥ LONG_INTERVAL_DAYS=365)?\n        │ yes → .word-mode: front-word-display only\n        │ no  → sentence front (also the universal fallback)\n```\n\nInterval retrieval is **platform-exclusive**: mobile uses only the AnkiDroid\nbridge (`ankiGetCardInterval()`, constructor + direct shapes, stub guard,\ntimeouts, 700 ms late-injection poll); desktop uses only AnkiConnect\n(`guiCurrentCard`→`cardsInfo`, `findCards` content-search fallback for the\nBrowse previewer, 500 ms fetch timeout). Anti-flash gate\n(`visibility:hidden` → reveal, 1200 ms safety cap). See `docs/adr/001-*`.\n\n### Back (`Card 1 - Back.template.anki`)\n\nSingle grid, content-driven height:\n\n```text\ntags-container (sticky)\ncard-container\n └── back-grid (1 col mobile; 2 col desktop via container + media queries)\n      ├── main-content: word-header (word-display-row + definition-box)\n      │                 sentence-block (sentence-japanese + translation + context)\n      └── side-content: picture-block · kanji notes · notes · extended accordion\n      └── source-footer\n```\n\nJS controllers (all idempotent under WebView DOM re-use): tags renderer,\nnative-only circular audio (`playCircularAudio` → sibling replay link click,\nre-tap debounce, ring pulse), frequency visualizer (tier → bar + stars),\ndefinition truncator (measure → `.is-truncated` → one-way `.is-expanded`),\nlightbox (backdrop-click / `Escape` close, alt preserved).\n\n### Style (`Card 1 - Style.css`)\n\nNumbered sections are the contract: §1 tokens/themes, §2 containers, §3\ntags, §4 grid + desktop overrides, §5 front type, §5b word-mode swap, §6\nheader/meta/definition, **§6b Definition Compactor** (first dictionary, ≤2\nsenses, no appendices, `.primary-definition`-scoped), §6c truncator (3-line\ncap + fade + chevron), §7 audio rings, §8 sentence/translation, §9\nzero-reflow ruby, §10 media/lightbox, §11 accordion/footer, §12 listening,\n§13 mobile, §14 deletable Fuji backdrop, §15 reduced motion.\n\n### Tooling\n\n```text\nfetch_anki_fields.py → .anki_fields.json (gitignored, read-only dump)\nsync_to_anki.py      → snapshot backups/<ts>/, push Front/Back/CSS\nrelease_apkg.py      → exportPackage deck → dist/*.apkg (gitignored)\nverify               → local quality gate (tests only, no side effects)\nfinish.sh            → verify → stamp → sync → export → commit → push → release\ntests/               → test_compactor.py + test_templates.py + test_layout.py\n```\n\nFields are **not** a repo artifact: the Anki UI owns them; agents bootstrap\nvia `fetch_anki_fields.py` every session.\n\n## Diagrams\n\nProduced only where prose is ambiguous. The two graphs above (front-mode\ndecision, back grid) are the standing set; sequence/state/entity diagrams\nare omitted — no complex async choreography, state machine, or persistent\nentity graph exists beyond what is shown.\n\n## Key decisions (summaries; full records in `docs/adr/`)\n\n- **001** Mature interval via platform-exclusive live read (no\n  `{{Interval}}`, no new fields/add-ons; mobile never touches AnkiConnect).\n- **002** Definition Compactor as structural CSS (dictionary-agnostic,\n  `.primary-definition`-scoped; extended definition untouched).\n- **003** Native-only audio delegation (no `new Audio()`; sibling replay\n  link; debounce; visually-hidden-not-`display:none` source).\n- **004** Single-command release (`finish.sh`; `verify` as the side-effect-free\n  subset; snapshots before overwrite).\n\n## Evolution rule\n\nSmall change → implement normally. Moderate change → update this file\n(+ ADR when justified) first, then add tasks. Major change → stop feature\nwork, run an explicit architecture-change effort with a migration plan.\n",

{
  "content": "# ADR 001 — Mature Word Mode interval retrieval\n\nDate: 2026-09 (records the as-built decision).\n\n## Context\n\nAnti-overlearning requires the front to show only the Expression once a\ncard is mature (interval ≥ `LONG_INTERVAL_DAYS`). No `{{Interval}}` marker\nexists, and new fields / add-ons / review-time Python are all rejected.\n\n## Options considered\n\n1. Bake the interval into a field at mining time — stale the next day.\n2. Guess the card id from the URL — no stable id is exposed.\n3. Single live-read path for both platforms — mobile has no AnkiConnect and\n   the refused fetch surfaces as `Failed to load 'downloadfile.bin'`.\n4. **Platform-exclusive live read (chosen).**\n\n## Decision\n\nDesktop reads AnkiConnect only (`guiCurrentCard`→`cardsInfo`, `findCards`\ncontent-search fallback for the Browse previewer). Mobile reads the AnkiDroid\nJS bridge only (`ankiGetCardInterval()`, both constructor and direct shapes,\nstub guard, timeouts, short late-injection poll). Listening fronts skip all\nretrieval. Any failure degrades to the sentence front behind an anti-flash\ngate.\n\n## Consequences\n\nPreview screens without a bridge correctly show the sentence front. Two\nplatform paths must both be covered by `test_templates.py` §8b on every\nchange.\n",

  "content": "# ADR 002 — Definition Compactor as structural CSS\n\n## Context\n\nYomitan mines the full multi-dictionary gloss into `Definition`, far too\nverbose for the main card, while `Extended definition` must stay complete.\n\n## Options considered\n\n1. Trim at mining time (Yomitan settings/handlers) — fragile per-dictionary,\n   lost on re-mine.\n2. Trim in JS at render time — flash of full text, more failure modes.\n3. **Structural CSS hiding (chosen).**\n\n## Decision\n\nCSS §6b collapses to the first dictionary, ≤2 senses, no appendices, matched\non structure (`data-sc-*`, glossary lists) — never dictionary names — and\nscoped strictly to `.primary-definition`.\n\n## Consequences\n\nDictionary-agnostic and flash-free; Yomitan markup drift is caught by\n`test_compactor.py` fixtures taken from real mined cards.\n",
  "filePath": "/home/mohamed/Desktop/Github/anki-japanese-template/docs/adr/002-definition-compactor-css.md"
}

{
  "content": "# ADR 003 — Native-only audio delegation\n\n## Context\n\nCustom circular buttons (`文` / `言葉`) must play audio on Desktop and\nAnkiDroid without overlapping playback.\n\n## Options considered\n\n1. HTML5 `new Audio(filename)` — AnkiDroid render links carry no usable\n   filename (old builds emit `playsound:q:0`), causing bogus-file loads.\n2. Nest the replay link inside `<button>` — invalid HTML, Android WebView\n   mishandles taps.\n3. **`playCircularAudio` delegating to the sibling native replay link\n   (chosen).**\n\n## Decision\n\nThe `.raw-audio-source` span lives **outside** the button, visually hidden\nbut kept in layout (never `display:none`, which breaks programmatic\n`.click()`). Every play path resets state first; re-taps on the playing\nbutton are ignored (native audio can't be stopped); the ring is a decorative\npulse.\n\n## Consequences\n\nNo filenames are ever constructed in JS. Markup/JS contract is pinned by\n`test_templates.py` §§2–3b.\n",
  "filePath": "/home/mohamed/Desktop/Github/anki-japanese-template/docs/adr/003-native-audio-delegation.md"
}

{
  "content": "# ADR 004 — Single-command release with pre-sync snapshots\n\n## Context\n\nMulti-step endings (test → sync → export → commit → push → release) were\ndropped late in sessions when run manually.\n\n## Decision\n\n`finish.sh` runs the full chain and stops on first failure; `./verify` is\nthe side-effect-free subset it delegates to. `sync_to_anki.py` snapshots the\nlive Anki state to `backups/<timestamp>/` (microsecond stamps) before\noverwriting and aborts on empty live state. `release_apkg.py` uses the only\nverified export action (`exportPackage`) into gitignored `dist/`; the apkg\nships as a GitHub Release asset, never in the repo. No-op runs never publish.\n\n## Consequences\n\nOne command to remember; snapshots make Anki-side overwrites recoverable;\ntag, tree, and live templates agree (CSS header is stamped before sync).\n",
  "filePath": "/home/mohamed/Desktop/Github/anki-japanese-template/docs/adr/004-single-command-release.md"
}

{
  "content": "# AGENTS.md — agent entry point\n\nModern, ultra-compact Japanese sentence-mining note type for Anki.\nDesktop: Arch Linux Qt6 WebEngine (widescreen, dense). Mobile: Galaxy A50\nAnkiDroid WebKit (ultra-compact). This file is the entry point — the\nmethodology lives in the repository structure, not in this file.\n\n## Authority hierarchy\n\n```text\nPRODUCT.md             → what the product must do\nMVP.md                 → current scope (agents never widen it unasked)\nARCHITECTURE.md        → technical structure (+ docs/adr/ for lasting decisions)\nQUALITY.md             → properties that must remain true\nTEST_STRATEGY.md       → how those properties are verified\nIMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md → task graph + status (work the next READY task)\nAGENTS.md (this file)  → how to navigate and operate within all of the above\n```\n\n`CODE ≠ specification.` Code implements the specs; tests enforce them.\n\n## Read order\n\n1. `PRODUCT.md` + `MVP.md` — what and what-now.\n2. `ARCHITECTURE.md` (+ relevant `docs/adr/`) — how it is structured.\n3. `QUALITY.md` + `TEST_STRATEGY.md` — invariants + enforcement.\n4. `IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md` — current work and dependencies.\n5. Session bootstrap below, then the existing code.\n\n## Development loop (the repeating unit)\n\n```text\nSELECT READY TASK → UNDERSTAND → SMALL CONTRACT → TEST/CHECK → IMPLEMENT\n  → TARGETED CHECKS → ./verify → UPDATE STATE/DOCS (only if changed)\n  → COMMIT → CI → NEXT READY TASK\n```\n\n- Work the next task whose dependencies are COMPLETE; never `Build the MVP`.\n- Split the task into the smallest meaningful contracts (validated bricks);\n  write the test/check before or alongside each brick, then implement only\n  enough to satisfy it.\n- Targeted suites while iterating, `./verify` before declaring done.\n- Bugs branch inside the loop: diagnose → fix → **add regression test** →\n  verify → continue. After ~3 blind retries, stop and diagnose root cause\n  (stronger model, then human); fix the cause class (spec, invariant, test,\n  architecture, tooling) so the system gets stronger.\n- MVP completion adds an end-to-end validation (real user journey in\n  `MVP.md`) before release; post-MVP changes go product decision →\n  architecture reassessment → tasks → loop → release.\n\n## Operating rules\n\n### 0. Field-Name Bootstrap (fields live in Anki, never in the repo)\n\nNote-type fields are managed **exclusively inside the Anki UI**. At session\nstart, **before** any template work, run `python3 fetch_anki_fields.py` and\nread the gitignored `.anki_fields.json` for exact names/descriptions. If\nAnki/Anki-Connect is unreachable, **stop and ask the user to start Anki** —\nnever guess, invent, or reuse field names from memory or chat history.\n\n### 1. Local files are the single source of truth\n\nAll edits happen in the local `.template.anki` / `.css` files. Never instruct\nedits inside the Anki UI. Tooling is stdlib-only Python.\n\n### 2. Release workflow (one command, not five)\n\nAfter **every** template/CSS modification, finish with exactly one command:\n\n```bash\n./finish.sh \"<semantic commit message>\"\n# --local: sync + export + commit only · --minor: bump v1.x.0\n# --prompt \"text\": archive prompt (rule 3) before anything runs\n```\n\nChain (stops on first failure): `0.` `./verify` (side-effect-free gate) →\n`1.` version stamp → `2.` `sync_to_anki.py` (pre-sync snapshot to gitignored\n`backups/<timestamp>/`) → `3.` `release_apkg.py` (deck\n`My Life Decks::Japanese::anki-japanese-template` via `exportPackage` to\ngitignored `dist/*.apkg`) → `4.` commit → `5.` `gh release create`\n(auto-bump tag) → `6.` push. The apkg ships as a Release asset, never in the\nrepo. Do not skip, reorder, or substitute steps.\n\n### 3. Prompt archiving\n\nArchive every new user prompt to `chat_history/opencode_prompts.txt`\n(prompt text + `---` separator) before/with its commit — preferably via\n`./finish.sh --prompt \"<user prompt>\" \"<message>\"`.\n\n### 4. Technical constraints (summaries; full rules in QUALITY.md)\n\nZero-reflow furigana (hidden, hover/tap reveal, absolute ruby) · native-only\ncircular audio (`文`/`言葉`, sibling replay link, debounce) · Mature Word\nMode (`LONG_INTERVAL_DAYS = 365`, platform-exclusive live interval read,\nsentence fallback, listening untouched, anti-flash gate) · no debug badges\nor verbose labels · vanilla scoped JS resilient to WebView DOM re-use.\n\n## File map\n\n`Card 1 - Front.template.anki` (front modes + Mature Word Mode) ·\n`Card 1 - Back.template.anki` (grid, audio, lightbox) ·\n`Card 1 - Style.css` (themes, layout, compactor §6b, truncator §6c) ·\n`fetch_anki_fields.py` · `sync_to_anki.py` · `release_apkg.py` · `verify` ·\n`finish.sh` · `tests/` · `docs/adr/` · `chat_history/` · `dist/` + `backups/`\n(gitignored).\n",
  "filePath": "/home/mohamed/Desktop/Github/anki-japanese-template/AGENTS.md"
}

Model: Japanese Note type (Sentence card by Default) (20 fields) -> .anki_fields.json
   0. Expression — Term expressed (without inflections ! ie conjugation of verbs) using maximal kanji (learned the hard way). If kanji expression is not available, kana is used. This is the best field to put first for handling search and duplicates.
   1. Definition
   2. Kanji Notes — Empty by default. This field will contain any info about the kanji(s) of the expression you deem necessary: for example radicals, mnemonics, meaning of the kanji, jukugo, readings etc, for kanji n°1 to 4
   3. Source — URL, show name, book name, etc
   4. Sentence
   5. Sentence (furigana)
   6. Sentence Audio
   7. Translation — maybe also include a literal english translation
   8. Picture
   9. context — The whole paragrah/diologue. Typically in books, added manually
  10. Notes — everthing else is here, grammar, other words defintions
  11. Word Audio
  12. Pitch Accent
  13. furigana — I try to use the plain formatting for furigana as much as possible since it is seems more intuitive for me
  14. reading — Kana reading for the term, or empty for terms where the expression is the reading. This field should not contain any kanji.
  15. cloze-prefix
  16. cloze-body — Original inflected term as it appeared before being reduced to dictionary form by Yomitan.
  17. cloze-suffix
  18. Frequency — Frequency of the cloze body
  19. Extended definition — Here we put the full Yomitan defintionin japanese

```

# Task: Tighten and improve the Anki Japanese card design

Repository: https://github.com/mansourvery-hub/anki-japanese-template

## Objective

Improve the visual design of the Anki Japanese card template so that it uses significantly less wasted vertical space while preserving readability, hierarchy, responsiveness, furigana support, and existing functionality. The main goal is to make the cards feel dense, deliberate, and information-efficient. [full spec implemented: content-driven sizing, tightened spacing tokens, headword/sentence/definition/secondary typography hierarchy, 3-line definition cap, compact listening view, tighter accordion/source, mobile + desktop verified]

— turns accumulated work into a release.\n4. **Evolution loop** — used when adding features or changing architecture.\n5. **Failure/escalation rules** — rules that apply inside the development loop follows.\n\n# The actual system\n\n```text\n                     PROJECT INITIALIZATION\n                              │\n        ┌─────────────────────┼─────────────────────┐\n        ↓                     ↓                     ↓\n   PRODUCT.md             MVP.md              ARCHITECTURE.md\n        │                     │                     │\n        └─────────────────────┼─────────────────────┘\n                              ↓\n                    ENGINEERING BASELINE\n                              │\n             ┌────────────────┴────────────────┐\n             ↓                                 ↓\n       QUALITY / RULES                   IMPLEMENTATION\n       + verification                       GRAPH\n             │                                 │\n             └────────────────┬────────────────┘\n                              ↓\n                    ┌─────────────────┐\n                    │ DEVELOPMENT LOOP│\n                    └─────────────────┘\n                              │\n                    repeat until MVP\n                              │\n                              ↓\n                            RELEASE\n                              │\n                              ↓\n                    ┌─────────────────┐\n                    │ EVOLUTION LOOP  │\n                    └─────────────────┘\n                              │\n                         repeat forever\n```\n\nThat's the structure I'd build the whole methodology around.\n\n---\n\n# PART I — Project initialization\n\nThis starts when you have:\n\n> \"I have an idea for an app.\"\n\nThis phase happens once, although its artifacts can later be revised.\n\n---\n\n## 1. Product definition\n\n### WHAT\n\nYou explore the product with a strong reasoning model.\n\nThe result is the durable product specification.\n\n### WHO\n\nYou + reasoning model.\n\n### ARTIFACT\n\n```text\nPRODUCT.md\n```\n\nIt contains:\n\n```text\nProblem\nTarget users\nCore user journeys\nFunctional requirements\nUX requirements\nConstraints\nNon-goals\nImportant assumptions\nOpen questions\n```\n\nThe important point is:\n\n**PRODUCT.md is the authoritative source for what the product is supposed to do.**\n\nNot the architecture documents.\n\nNot the implementation plan.\n\nNot the agent instructions.\n\n### WHAT YOU DECIDE\n\nYou approve the product definition.\n\n### OUTPUT\n\nA durable product specification that survives the end of the conversation.\n\n---\n\n# 2. Define the MVP\n\nThis should absolutely produce a persistent file.\n\nI would use:\n\n```text\nMVP.md\n```\n\n### WHY\n\nBecause:\n\n```text\nPRODUCT.md\n```\n\nanswers:\n\n> What is this product?\n\nwhile:\n\n```text\nMVP.md\n```\n\nanswers:\n\n> What are we actually building **right now**?\n\nThat distinction is extremely useful.\n\n### MVP.md contains\n\n```text\nMVP goal\n\nCore user journey(s)\n\nIncluded capabilities\n\nExcluded capabilities\n\nAcceptance criteria\n\nKnown limitations\n\nDeferred features\n```\n\nFor example:\n\n```text\nPRODUCT\n├── import configs\n├── edit configs\n├── validate configs\n├── export configs\n├── multiple providers\n├── cloud sync\n├── collaboration\n└── analytics\n\nMVP\n├── import configs\n├── edit configs\n├── validate configs\n└── export configs\n\nDEFERRED\n├── cloud sync\n├── collaboration\n└── analytics\n```\n\n### WHO\n\nYou + reasoning model.\n\n### IMPORTANT\n\nYou approve this.\n\nAn agent should **not independently decide the MVP scope while coding**.\n\n### OUTPUT\n\nA persistent statement of:\n\n> This is the scope we are trying to make work first.\n\n---\n\n# 3. Technical architecture\n\nNow we move from:\n\n> What are we building?\n\nto:\n\n> How should the software be structured?\n\nHere I agree with your criticism.\n\nDo **not** force every project to contain:\n\n```text\nDATA_MODEL.md\nAPI_CONTRACTS.md\nTECHNICAL_DECISIONS.md\n...\n```\n\nThat's too rigid.\n\nInstead:\n\n### Mandatory\n\n```text\nARCHITECTURE.md\n```\n\n### Optional documents\n\nThe architecture-designing model decides what additional artifacts are actually useful.\n\nFor example:\n\n```text\nARCHITECTURE.md\nDATA_MODEL.md\nAPI_CONTRACTS.md\nSTATE_MODEL.md\nSECURITY.md\nPERFORMANCE.md\nINTEGRATION.md\nDOMAIN_X.md\n...\n```\n\nA tiny CLI may need almost nothing.\n\nA database-heavy backend may benefit greatly from `DATA_MODEL.md`.\n\nA stateful Flutter application may need a `STATE_MODEL.md`.\n\nAn API-heavy application may need `API_CONTRACTS.md`.\n\nA browser extension integrating Yomitan may need integration-specific documentation.\n\n### This is the instruction to the architecture model\n\nNot:\n\n> Create these seven files.\n\nInstead:\n\n> Design the technical architecture for the MVP. Create `ARCHITECTURE.md` as the authoritative architectural document. Create additional technical documents only where they contain substantial information that would otherwise make `ARCHITECTURE.md` unwieldy or where a domain needs an independently maintained contract/specification.\n\nThat gives the senior model freedom.\n\n---\n\n# 4. Diagrams\n\nDiagrams are **tools**, not mandatory deliverables.\n\nThe architecture model should create diagrams when they clarify something that prose doesn't.\n\nUseful candidates:\n\n### Architecture/component diagram\n\nUseful almost always for anything beyond a tiny application.\n\nShows:\n\n```text\nUI\n ↓\nApplication layer\n ↓\nDomain\n ↓\nRepositories\n ↓\nPersistence\n```\n\n### Data/entity diagram\n\nOnly when relationships between persistent entities matter.\n\n### Sequence diagram\n\nUseful for complicated interactions.\n\nFor example:\n\n```text\nUser\n ↓\nFlutter\n ↓\nAPI\n ↓\nLLM provider\n ↓\nstream\n ↓\nUI\n```\n\n### State diagram\n\nVery useful when the application contains complicated state machines.\n\n### Dependency graph\n\nExtremely useful for **implementation planning**, not necessarily architecture.\n\n### Task graph\n\nThis belongs to implementation planning.\n\nSo don't turn diagrams into a checklist like:\n\n> Every project must have 6 diagrams.\n\nInstead:\n\n> Produce the minimum diagrams necessary to make important structure and behavior unambiguous.\n\n---\n\n# 5. Architectural decisions\n\nYou also identified an important problem.\n\n`TECHNICAL_DECISIONS.md` shouldn't necessarily exist.\n\nFor a simple project, decisions can simply live in:\n\n```text\nARCHITECTURE.md\n```\n\nFor projects with many meaningful decisions, use ADRs:\n\n```text\ndocs/adr/\n    001-storage.md\n    002-state-management.md\n    003-provider-abstraction.md\n```\n\nAn ADR is useful when a decision has:\n\n```text\nmultiple plausible alternatives\n+\nmeaningful consequences\n+\nlong-term relevance\n```\n\nNot:\n\n> We decided to name this class `ConfigParser`.\n\nThat's not an ADR.\n\n---\n\n# 6. Define engineering constraints\n\nNow comes the part the earlier workflow badly misplaced.\n\nCreate:\n\n```text\nQUALITY.md\n```\n\nThis answers:\n\n> What properties must remain true?\n\nFor example:\n\n```text\nArchitecture invariants\n- Presentation layer does not access persistence.\n\nData invariants\n- Imported configurations are normalized before storage.\n\nReliability requirements\n- Malformed external input must not crash the application.\n\nPerformance requirements\n- Normal config import should complete within X.\n\nSecurity requirements\n- API keys must never enter logs.\n```\n\nBut there's another document we need.\n\n---\n\n# 7. Define the testing/verification strategy\n\nOnly **now** introduce:\n\n```text\nTEST_STRATEGY.md\n```\n\nYou were absolutely right that I previously pulled this document out of nowhere.\n\nIts job is to answer:\n\n> How do we mechanically prove that the important properties in QUALITY.md remain true?\n\nFor example:\n\n| Requirement                       | Enforcement               |\n| --------------------------------- | ------------------------- |\n| UI cannot access DB               | architecture test         |\n| Parser removes `<style>`          | unit test                 |\n| Config schema is valid            | schema/validation test    |\n| API response conforms to contract | integration/contract test |\n| Import handles 100k entries       | benchmark                 |\n| User can import → edit → export   | E2E test                  |\n\nThis relationship is important:\n\n```text\nQUALITY.md\n     │\n     │ says WHAT must remain true\n     ↓\nTEST_STRATEGY.md\n     │\n     │ says HOW it is verified\n     ↓\ntests / linters / benchmarks / CI\n```\n\nThat's the clean separation.\n\n---\n\n# 8. Build the implementation plan\n\nNow we're ready to ask:\n\n> What do we actually build first?\n\nAnd yes, **the graph should be first-class**, not just prose buried inside Markdown.\n\nI would therefore have:\n\n```text\nIMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md\nIMPLEMENTATION_GRAPH.md\n```\n\nor simply a graph embedded in the implementation plan if you want fewer files.\n\nFor your style, I'd probably use:\n\n```text\nIMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md\n```\n\ncontaining:\n\n1. task definitions\n2. dependency graph\n3. execution status\n\nSo the graph is not a substitute for the plan; it is one part of it.\n\nFor example:\n\n```text\n                    ┌───────────────┐\n                    │ Project shell │\n                    └───────┬───────┘\n                            │\n                     ┌──────┴──────┐\n                     ↓             ↓\n              Data model       UI shell\n                     │             │\n                     ↓             ↓\n               Repository      Editor UI\n                     │             │\n                     └──────┬──────┘\n                            ↓\n                       Validation\n                            │\n                            ↓\n                     Import / Export\n                            │\n                            ↓\n                       E2E journey\n                            │\n                            ↓\n                          MVP\n```\n\nEach node is a task/slice.\n\nThe graph tells us:\n\n```text\nwhat depends on what\n```\n\nThe plan tells us:\n\n```text\nwhat each node actually means\n```\n\n---\n\n# 9. The plan is not a todo list\n\nThis is an important distinction.\n\nBad:\n\n```text\n[ ] Build frontend\n[ ] Build backend\n[ ] Add authentication\n[ ] Add settings\n```\n\nGood:\n\n```text\nT1 — Configuration domain model\nT2 — Configuration repository\nT3 — Config import parser\nT4 — Config editor\nT5 — Validation pipeline\nT6 — Export pipeline\nT7 — Import/edit/export E2E journey\n```\n\nWith dependencies:\n\n```text\nT1 → T2 → T3\n          ↓\n         T5\nT1 → T4 → T5\n          ↓\n         T6\n          ↓\n         T7\n```\n\nNow an agent can reason about what is ready.\n\n---\n\n# 10. Repository agent context\n\nNow we create:\n\n```text\nAGENTS.md\n```\n\nBut its role is much narrower than I previously made it.\n\nIt should **not contain the entire engineering methodology**.\n\nIt should be the agent's entry point:\n\n```text\nRead PRODUCT.md for product requirements.\n\nRead MVP.md for current scope.\n\nRead ARCHITECTURE.md for system architecture.\n\nRead QUALITY.md for invariants.\n\nRead TEST_STRATEGY.md for verification strategy.\n\nRead IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md for current work.\n\nFollow repository tooling and verification requirements.\n\nDo not modify architectural constraints without updating\nthe authoritative architectural documentation.\n```\n\nThat's enough.\n\nThen domain-specific agent guidance can live near the domain:\n\n```text\nsrc/yomitan/AGENTS.md\nsrc/parser/AGENTS.md\n```\n\nwhen actually necessary.\n\n---\n\n# PART II — The real development loop\n\nEverything above establishes the project.\n\nNow we enter the **repeating loop**.\n\nThis is the core of the whole system.\n\n```text\nSELECT READY TASK\n        ↓\nUNDERSTAND TASK\n        ↓\nDEFINE SMALL CONTRACT\n        ↓\nWRITE TEST / CHECK\n        ↓\nIMPLEMENT\n        ↓\nLOCAL VERIFICATION\n        ↓\nDEBUG IF NECESSARY\n        ↓\nUPDATE STATE\n        ↓\nCOMMIT\n        ↓\nCI\n        ↓\nMERGE\n        ↓\nNEXT READY TASK\n```\n\nThis repeats dozens or hundreds of times.\n\nThat is what was missing from the previous version.\n\n---\n\n# 11. Select the next task\n\nThe agent does **not** receive:\n\n> \"Build the MVP.\"\n\nIt receives:\n\n> \"Work on the next ready task.\"\n\nThe agent reads:\n\n```text\nIMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md\n```\n\nand finds a task whose dependencies are complete.\n\nFor example:\n\n```text\nT3 — Config parser\n\nStatus: READY\n\nDependencies:\nT1 complete\nT2 complete\n```\n\nIt becomes the current task.\n\nYou don't need to paste the entire development methodology into the prompt every time because the repository itself contains it.\n\n---\n\n# 12. Define the small contract\n\nThis is where the video's \"small validated bricks\" actually enters the workflow.\n\nThe agent examines the task and identifies the smallest meaningful contract.\n\nExample:\n\nTask:\n\n> Implement Yomitan definition parser.\n\nInstead of immediately writing the full parser:\n\n```text\nHTML\n ↓\nparser\n ↓\ndefinition\n```\n\nthe agent identifies contracts such as:\n\n```text\nC1: locate definition node\nC2: remove irrelevant nodes\nC3: preserve required markup\nC4: extract definition text\nC5: return normalized representation\n```\n\nNow we get:\n\n```text\ntask\n ↓\nsmall contracts\n ↓\ntests\n ↓\nimplementation\n```\n\nThat is the practical meaning of a \"validated brick.\"\n\n---\n\n# 13. Write the test for the brick\n\nThe agent creates the smallest appropriate test.\n\nExample:\n\n```text\nInput:\n<div>\n  definition\n  <style>huge CSS...</style>\n</div>\n\nExpected:\ndefinition\n```\n\nThe test becomes the executable contract.\n\nThe important point is:\n\n**this happens before or alongside implementation of the brick, not after the entire feature is completed.**\n\n---\n\n# 14. Implement the brick\n\nThe agent now implements only enough code to satisfy the contract.\n\nThen:\n\n```text\nsmall test\n    ↓\nimplementation\n    ↓\nsmall test passes\n```\n\nThen the next brick:\n\n```text\nC1\n ↓\nC2\n ↓\nC3\n ↓\nC4\n```\n\nYou are progressively constructing the feature from validated components.\n\n---\n\n# 15. What happens to `./verify`?\n\nNow its place is clear.\n\nThere are **two levels of verification**.\n\n### Local/inner verification\n\nDuring implementation:\n\n```text\ntargeted test\n```\n\nanswers:\n\n> Does this brick work?\n\nThen increasingly broad checks:\n\n```text\nunit tests\nintegration tests\netc.\n```\n\nanswer:\n\n> Did my change break related things?\n\n### `./verify`\n\n`./verify` means:\n\n> Run the repository's complete mandatory local quality gate.\n\nExample:\n\n```text\n./verify\n ├── formatting\n ├── lint\n ├── static/type analysis\n ├── unit tests\n ├── integration tests\n ├── architecture checks\n └── other mandatory checks\n```\n\nAn agent should normally run targeted checks while iterating and then `./verify` before declaring the task complete.\n\nThat makes the hierarchy:\n\n```text\ntiny test\n    ↓\ntargeted test suite\n    ↓\n./verify\n    ↓\nCI\n```\n\n---\n\n# 16. Where regression tests fit\n\nNow regression testing has a proper location.\n\nSuppose the parser bug escapes:\n\n```text\n<style> leaked into result\n```\n\nThe agent fixes it.\n\nThen the development loop includes:\n\n```text\nBUG\n ↓\ndiagnose\n ↓\nfix\n ↓\nadd regression test\n ↓\nverify\n ↓\ncommit\n```\n\nThe regression test becomes part of the permanent repository test suite.\n\nSo regression tests are **not a separate phase**.\n\nThey're a rule inside the debugging branch of the development loop:\n\n```text\nnormal development\n       ↓\nbug discovered\n       ↓\nfix bug\n       ↓\nadd regression test\n       ↓\ncontinue normal development\n```\n\n---\n\n# 17. Completion of a task\n\nOnce implementation and verification are successful, the agent updates state.\n\nFor example:\n\n```text\nIMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md\n\nT3 — Config parser\nStatus: COMPLETE\n```\n\nThe agent may also update technical documentation **only if something actually changed**.\n\nFor example:\n\n```text\narchitecture changed\n→ update ARCHITECTURE.md\n\nnew invariant introduced\n→ update QUALITY.md\n\nnew contract introduced\n→ update relevant contract/spec\n\nbug fixed\n→ add regression test\n```\n\nNo pointless documentation churn.\n\n---\n\n# 18. Git happens here\n\nGit is therefore not \"step 13.\"\n\nIt is part of the development loop.\n\n```text\ntask\n ↓\nimplementation\n ↓\ntests\n ↓\n./verify\n ↓\ncommit\n ↓\npush\n ↓\nCI\n ↓\nmerge\n```\n\nFor your solo workflow:\n\n```text\nmain\n│\n├── feature/task-123\n│\n└── feature/task-124\n```\n\nAgents work on task branches.\n\n`main` is protected and should remain healthy.\n\n---\n\n# 19. What should agents do with Git?\n\nI'd give agents permission to:\n\n```text\ncreate branch\ncommit\npush branch\n```\n\nbut **not automatically merge to main**.\n\nThe merge can be your lightweight human control point.\n\nFor multiple agents, the rule becomes:\n\n```text\nAgent A → branch A\nAgent B → branch B\nAgent C → branch C\n```\n\nNever let five agents independently modify the same working tree.\n\nA shared branch/worktree is an unnecessary source of collisions.\n\n---\n\n# 20. CI\n\nAfter the agent pushes:\n\n```text\nGitHub\n ↓\nCI\n ↓\n./verify\n ↓\npass/fail\n```\n\nThis is the second independent verification layer.\n\nLocal verification answers:\n\n> Did the agent's environment pass?\n\nCI answers:\n\n> Does the repository pass in a clean environment?\n\nThis catches things such as:\n\n```text\nforgotten file\nundeclared dependency\nplatform mismatch\nenvironment-specific issue\ngenerated files missing\n```\n\n---\n\n# PART III — MVP completion\n\nEventually the graph reaches:\n\n```text\nall MVP tasks complete\n```\n\nBut you're not done merely because all checkboxes are green.\n\nNow run an MVP validation.\n\n---\n\n# 21. MVP validation\n\nThis is a specific checkpoint.\n\nAsk:\n\n```text\nCan a real user complete the core journey defined in MVP.md?\n```\n\nThis is where E2E testing becomes particularly valuable.\n\nFor example:\n\n```text\nLaunch\n ↓\nImport config\n ↓\nEdit config\n ↓\nValidate\n ↓\nExport\n ↓\nOpen exported config\n```\n\nThe E2E test proves the **system**, not individual components.\n\nNow you have:\n\n```text\nunit/brick tests\n+\nintegration tests\n+\narchitecture checks\n+\nE2E\n+\n./verify\n```\n\nand the MVP acceptance criteria.\n\n---\n\n# 22. Release\n\nNow:\n\n```text\nMVP\n ↓\nrelease candidate\n ↓\nCI\n ↓\nversion/tag\n ↓\nrelease\n```\n\nYou might create:\n\n```text\nCHANGELOG.md\n```\n\nand release notes.\n\nFor a personal app, don't turn this into enterprise release management.\n\n---\n\n# PART IV — After MVP\n\nThis is where your earlier workflow jumped awkwardly.\n\nAfter the MVP, you enter an **evolution loop**.\n\n```text\nnew feature idea\n       ↓\nproduct decision\n       ↓\nupdate PRODUCT.md if necessary\n       ↓\nupdate MVP/roadmap scope\n       ↓\narchitecture impact?\n       ↓\nupdate architecture if necessary\n       ↓\nadd implementation tasks\n       ↓\ndevelopment loop\n       ↓\nrelease\n```\n\nThis repeats indefinitely.\n\n---\n\n# 23. Architecture is not frozen\n\nThis is crucial.\n\nYou don't do:\n\n```text\narchitecture\n ↓\nfreeze forever\n```\n\nInstead:\n\n```text\narchitecture\n ↓\nimplementation\n ↓\nreal-world knowledge\n ↓\nnew requirement\n ↓\narchitecture reassessment\n```\n\nWhen a new feature doesn't fit:\n\n### Small change\n\nImplement it normally.\n\n### Moderate architectural change\n\nUpdate `ARCHITECTURE.md`, possibly an ADR, then create tasks.\n\n### Major change\n\nStop normal feature development and create an explicit architecture-change effort.\n\nFor example:\n\n```text\nFlutter state architecture\n        ↓\nnew requirement exposes limitations\n        ↓\ndecision\n        ↓\nmigration plan\n        ↓\nmigration tasks\n        ↓\nnormal development resumes\n```\n\n---\n\n# The complete repository\n\nI'd therefore simplify your proposed repository to:\n\n```text\nPRODUCT.md\nMVP.md\nARCHITECTURE.md\nQUALITY.md\nTEST_STRATEGY.md\nIMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md\nAGENTS.md\n\nsrc/\ntests/\n\ndocs/\n    adr/              # only if useful\n    architecture/    # only if useful\n    domain/           # only if useful\n\n.github/\n    workflows/\n\nverify\n```\n\nAnd potentially:\n\n```text\nDATA_MODEL.md\nAPI_CONTRACTS.md\nSTATE_MODEL.md\nPERFORMANCE.md\nSECURITY.md\n```\n\n**only when justified by the project.**\n\nThat gives the senior architecture model freedom without turning documentation into chaos.\n\n---\n\n# The authority hierarchy\n\nThis is something I would explicitly put into `AGENTS.md`.\n\n```text\nPRODUCT.md\n    ↓\nDefines product intent and requirements\n\nMVP.md\n    ↓\nDefines current scope\n\nARCHITECTURE.md\n    ↓\nDefines technical structure\n\nSpecialized technical docs\n    ↓\nDefine detailed contracts/models for their domain\n\nQUALITY.md\n    ↓\nDefines properties/invariants that must remain true\n\nTEST_STRATEGY.md\n    ↓\nDefines how those properties are mechanically verified\n\nIMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md\n    ↓\nDefines current implementation work and dependencies\n\nAGENTS.md\n    ↓\nExplains how an agent navigates and operates within all of this\n```\n\nAnd critically:\n\n```text\nCODE ≠ specification\n```\n\nCode implements the specifications.\n\nTests enforce important properties of the specifications.\n\n---\n\n# The agent's actual operating loop\n\nThis is what I think you were ultimately trying to get to.\n\nAn agent receives essentially:\n\n> **Implement the next ready task.**\n\nThat's it.\n\nThe repository tells it the rest.\n\nThe agent should then autonomously do:\n\n```text\n1. Read AGENTS.md\n2. Read relevant product/MVP requirements\n3. Read relevant architecture\n4. Read relevant quality rules\n5. Inspect existing code\n6. Select current task\n7. Identify its smallest contracts\n8. Add/update appropriate tests\n9. Implement\n10. Run targeted checks\n11. Run ./verify\n12. Fix failures\n13. Add regression tests when bugs were discovered\n14. Update implementation state\n15. Update technical docs only when necessary\n16. Commit\n17. Push\n18. CI verifies\n19. Task becomes complete\n20. Select next ready task\n```\n\nAnd **that** is the autonomous agentic development system.\n\n---\n\n# Failure handling lives inside this loop\n\nDon't create another numbered phase.\n\nThe development loop branches:\n\n```text\n                  TASK\n                    │\n              implement\n                    │\n                verify\n                    │\n           ┌────────┴────────┐\n           │                 │\n         PASS              FAIL\n           │                 │\n        finish          diagnose\n                             │\n                 ┌───────────┼───────────┐\n                 ↓           ↓           ↓\n             code bug   missing test   bad design/spec\n                 │           │           │\n                 └───────────┴───────────┘\n                             ↓\n                         fix cause\n                             ↓\n                       verify again\n```\n\nAfter repeated failure, escalate.\n\nI'd use something like:\n\n```text\nAttempt 1–2:\nagent investigates and fixes\n\nAttempt 3:\nagent must stop blind retrying and diagnose root cause\n\nIf unclear:\nstronger reasoning/debugging model\n\nIf still unclear:\nhuman decision\n```\n\nAnd when the root cause is identified:\n\n```text\nmissing specification\n→ improve specification\n\nmissing invariant\n→ add QUALITY rule\n\nmissing protection\n→ add test/linter/check\n\narchitecture problem\n→ update architecture + migration/task plan\n\ntooling problem\n→ improve tooling\n```\n\nThat's how the system gets stronger over time.\n\n---\n\n# What I would change from your current version\n\nThe biggest conceptual changes are:\n\n### Replace this:\n\n```text\nProduct\n↓\nArchitecture\n↓\nPlan\n↓\nBuild\n↓\nTesting\n↓\nGit\n↓\nRelease\n```\n\nwith:\n\n```text\nINITIALIZE PROJECT\n\nProduct\n↓\nMVP\n↓\nArchitecture\n↓\nQuality/invariants\n↓\nVerification strategy\n↓\nImplementation graph\n↓\nAgent context\n↓\n──────────────────────────\n      DEVELOPMENT LOOP\n──────────────────────────\nselect task\n↓\nsmall contract\n↓\ntest/check\n↓\nimplement\n↓\ntargeted verification\n↓\n./verify\n↓\nstate/docs\n↓\ncommit\n↓\nCI\n↓\nmerge\n↓\nnext task\n──────────────────────────\n\nMVP validation\n↓\nrelease\n\nthen repeatedly:\n\nFEATURE / CHANGE\n↓\nproduct decision\n↓\narchitecture reassessment\n↓\nimplementation graph\n↓\nDEVELOPMENT LOOP\n↓\nrelease\n```\n\nThat is the version I'd recommend you actually adopt.\n\nThe **most important thing** is that the \"agentic\" part isn't a clever prompt. It's the repository becoming a **self-describing, mechanically enforced development environment** where:\n\n```text\nrequirements\n→ architecture\n→ constraints\n→ tests\n→ implementation tasks\n→ automated verification\n→ git/CI\n```\n\nform one connected system.\n\nThat also resolves your concern about \"what do I tell the agent?\" You don't want to repeatedly inject a giant methodology prompt. You want the agent to enter a repository whose structure already encodes the methodology.\n\nI can turn this revised structure into a clean one-page/printable workflow document next.\n\n---\n\nok ready\n\n---",

- Whitespace-only definition/sentence left bordered/padded voids → removed in JS (front runs post-cloze-fixup, pre-reveal)
- New `QUALITY.md` rule: *no padding, border, or margin may survive an empty field*

Start Anki and say go — I'll re-run `finish.sh` to sync, export, commit, and release v1.4.2.
